Let's think of a powerful algorithm implementable in numpy: **Fourier phase retrieval + local subgradient**.

Phase retrieval details:
- p length n, sum 1.
- Choose FFT length L = next_pow2(2n-1) maybe >=2n-1. For linear convolution via FFT, c = ifft(|fft(p, L)|^2)[:2n-1]. This c real nonnegative (up to rounding).
- We want reduce max. We can define target magnitude spectrum satisfying desired c. One simple method: **peak shaving in autocorrelation domain**:
    c = current
    M = c.max()
    c_shaped = c.copy()
    c_shaped[c_shaped > M*tau] = M*tau  # flatten top
    # renormalize to sum 1 (since sum c=1) by scaling down? Actually if clip, sum decreases, scale up by 1/sum. This increases all, may raise. Alternatively add clipped excess uniformly to low lags.
    excess = np.maximum(c - cap,0).sum()
    c_shaped = np.minimum(c, cap)
    # distribute excess to entries below cap: c_shaped += excess * weights where weights = max(0, cap - c_shaped), normalized; or simply scale low entries to fill.
  Then set new amplitude spectrum A = sqrt(max(0, real(fft(c_shaped))))? Since c_shaped symmetric? We can symmetrize c_shaped = (c_shaped + reverse)/2 to ensure even. Its FFT real. Some values may be negative. Use A = np.sqrt(np.maximum(0, fft_real)).
- Keep phase from current P = fft(p, L) (or target not). new p = real(ifft(A * phase))[:n]. But support length n? If we only take first n, we ignore rest; the corresponding zero-padded sequence has mass outside, need renormalize within first n. This may destroy. Alternative enforce support by projecting: set p_full = ifft(...); p_full[n:] =0; p = p_full[:n]. Then clip >=0, normalize.
- This is like IR with support and nonnegativity. It may converge to p with c close to clipped flat.
- Use multiple target caps: cap = current M * factor (0.9,0.85) to push lower. If c_shaped sum scaling ensures sum1. If A has too much mass outside support, after support truncation sum may drop; normalize.
- We can also randomize phase: start with random phase (but nonnegative p requires phase constraints). Use phase from current to maintain.
- We can run for many iterations; cheap: one FFT, one IFFT per iter. For n=4096, ~0.01s, thousands.
- This method could find globally low max. Need verify no invalid due to negative: clip.

But does it maintain sum1? normalize. If support zero, fallback.

Another method: **Minimum c max via IROS on circular convolution**: Use length N=2n-1. Desired circular autocorrelation g = even extension of c? Pseudo for q_subproblem:
Input p (sum1), initial q (maybe p or previous q). Run T iterations:
   x = p ⊛ q (length L) via FFT
   Mq = x.max()
   active = indices where x > Mq - delta (or top `r`)
   g = average over active of columns of A: for each k, add p segment reversed, divide by count.
   Use mirror descent update for simplex: q <- softmax(-eta_g * cumulative_g) or AdaGrad:
       Gcum += g
       q = exp(-η * Gcum) normalized.
   Maybe also line search? Since f convex, mirror descent with step 1/sqrt(t) works. But scale of f? We can use step based on Lipschitz: ||g||∞≤1, η=1/sqrt(K?) maybe. Simpler: use **projected gradient** or **exponential weights with η schedule** and evaluate f. For each iteration compute f exact; if stuck, adjust eta.
   More efficient: Use **negative list**: maintain active constraints list of K active rows (those with high violation) and solve a small LP? For convex min max, there is **bender decomposition**: solve master LP over constraints accumulated; q from simple LP. Since constraints K large but accumulated maybe few. Could be very effective: maintain set of active lag constraints where p*q was high. Master problem: min t s.t. A_S q ≤ t, sum q=1, q≥0. With small |S|, LP easy. Then evaluate full p*q, add violated constraints (where >t). Repeat. This is **cutting plane** for convex f. It converges in few iterations because active set small. This gives much more accurate q than mirror descent and uses LP only on small constraints! Great.

Algorithm for q subproblem:
- Start with S = empty or top peaks of p*p.
- Loop:
   Solve min t s.t. A_S q ≤ t, sumq=1, q≥0. Here A_S rows are for lags in S: (p*q)_k ≤ t.
   Compute full vector v=p*q via FFT.
   v_max = v.max(); if v_max - t ≤ tol, q optimal for full f.
   Else add top violated indices to S (where v > t + tol_s). Maybe cap |S| at e.g. 1000; if too many, remove least violated? Keep top.
   Repeat limited iterations/time.

Phase retrieval algorithm:
```
p = current
P = rfft(p, L)
for it:
   c = ifft(P*P, L)[:2n-1]
   c = np.maximum(c.real,0) ? Convolution nonnegative but numerical.
   # shape c: reduce peaks, preserve sum
   cap = c.max()*target_factor (e.g. 0.98) or target_abs
   c_new = c.copy()
   over = c_new > cap
   amount = c_new[over].sum()-cap*over.sum()
   c_new[over]=cap
   # distribute amount to under entries proportional to headroom or uniformly
   head = cap - c_new
   if head.sum()>0: c_new += amount*head/head.sum()
   else: scale all down? then renormalize.
   c_new = np.maximum(c_new, 0); c_new /= c_new.sum()
   C = rfft(c_new, L)
   mag = np.sqrt(np.abs(C))
   phase = P / np.abs(P) (where abs>0)
   P = mag * phase
   q_full = irfft(P, L)
   # support projection: maybe take first n real part; but phase may cause negative.
   q = q_full[:n]
   q = np.maximum(q,0)
   # renormalize; if zero fallback
   q /= q.sum()
   # optionally blend with old p to maintain stability: p = (1-beta)*p + beta*q
```
This keeps phase close to current p. It may converge to c close to target capped. It can reduce peaks. But changing amplitude to flatten c might push support/nonneg badly. We can blend.

We can choose **target factor sequence** e.g. start cap=M*0.99, decrease slowly to maybe M*0.90. If target too low, projection may oscillate. We can use line search between old and new.

This method is global-ish and fast. Could produce improvements.

Could use **Adam** with gradient of smooth max. Steps:
- p sum1. c=conv(p,p). M=c.max(). score=2nM.
- Weights w = softmax(τ c - max) (all k). Adjust τ adaptively: if τ too large, weights concentrate; if too small, smooth. Maybe start τ = 20n/M? - grad = correlation(w,p) (length n). Since c_k max at some peaks, grad points to reduce p_i that contribute to peaks. But c_k all nonnegative; sliding weights.
- We want minimize M, so descend: p <- p - lr * grad (plus bias). But gradient of max can be large. We can use **normalized gradient**: grad = corr(w,p) - mean? To keep sum? Project after.
- Use **softmax update**: p_new ∝ p * exp(-η grad). This automatically nonnegative and sum1 if normalize. This is mirror descent. Step η maybe 0.05. Adam? Let's use **multiplicative updates**:
   p *= exp(-η * (grad - offset))? To maintain scale, then normalize. This can damp peaks. But if grad has baseline, multiply uniformly cancels after normalize. We can center grad by subtracting its min or mean to avoid overflow.
   `u = grad - np.min(grad)` (or percentile)

def solve_dir_minmax(p, initial_q=None, max_iter=20, max_rows=200, tol=1e-7):
    q = p if none
    active = top_idx(p*p, maybe where c close max)
    for it in range(max_iter):
       solve master with active
       if fail: break
       q = z[1:]
       if q invalid: break
       v = conv(p,q)  # length 2n-1, use FFT
       t_master = z[0]
       v_max = v.max()
       if v_max - t_master < tol:
           # optimal for full selected? Actually selected feasible; if no violated full constraints? Need check if all v ≤ t_master? v_max is full max. If v_max≤t_master+tol, q is global minimizer of max cross.
           break

       viol = np.where(v > t_master + add_tol)[0]
       if len(viol)>0:
           # add top min(max_rows - len(active), len(viol))

       else break

**Fast convolution functions**:
```
def next_pow2(x):
    size=1
    while size<x: size*=2
    return size

def fft_self_conv(a):
   n=a.size
   size=next_pow2(2*n-1)
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)
   return c[:2*n-1]

def fft_conv(a,b): # full
   n=len(a); m=len(b); size=next_pow2(n+m-1)
   return np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a,size)*np.fft.rfft(b,size), size)[:n+m-1]

def grad_correlate_weights(weights, a):
   # weights length 2n-1, a length n, return valid correlation ∑ weights[i+d] a[d]
   n=a.size; L=next_pow2(3*n-2)
   W=np.fft.rfft(weights,L)
   A=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],L)
   g=np.fft.irfft(W*A,L)
   return g[n-1:2*n-1]
```

def build_master_matrix(p, active, max_rows):
    n=len(p); rows=[]
    for k in active:
       row=np.zeros(n)
       start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)
       if end>=start:
           d = k - np.arange(start,end+1)
           row[start:end+1]=p[d]
       rows.append(row)
    A_row=np.vstack(rows)  # m x n
    # A_ub for z [t; q]: each row [-1, coeffs]
    # Equality ones.
```

Let's implement a phase candidate generator:
```
def phase_shorten(a, eta_phase=0.5, target_factor=0.98, iterations=20):
   p=a/n; L=next_pow2(2n-1); pad; P=rfft(pad,L)
   for it in range(iterations):
      c = ifft(rfft? P*P) real[:2n-1]
      M=c.max(); cap=M*target_factor
      c_new=c.copy(); over=c_new>cap; excess=c_new.sum()-cap*(2n-1)? no if only over.
      over_mask = c_new > cap
      excess = c_new[over_mask].sum() - cap*over_mask.sum()
      c_new[over_mask]=cap
      head=cap - c_new
      if head.sum()>0 and excess>0:
          c_new += excess*head/head.sum()
      c_new = np.maximum(c_new,0); c_new/=c_new.sum()
      C=fft(c_new,L)
      mag=np.sqrt(np.abs(C))
      phase=P/abs(P) (eps)
      P=mag*phase
      qpad=irfft(P,L)
      q=qpad[:n]
      q=np.maximum(q,0); qsum=q.sum(); if qsum<eps break
      q=q/qsum*n? sum n?
      # line search blend with current a?
      for alpha in [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8]:
         cand=(1-alpha)a+alpha q; normalize; fast; best
      if best improves, update a=best
   return a
```
This is heuristic. It may improve or not. We can call when smooth no improve for long, for e.g., 30s.

But phase modification of `mag` using |C| where C complex can be wrong? It tries to match spectrum magnitude of desired c, not phase. Phase from current P. This is common. Could help. Need ensure P has correct symmetry for real output: Using rfft handles Hermitian. `phase = P / sqrt(P.real^2+P.imag^2)`. For P=0, phase=1. Multiplying mag by phase yields complex; irfft real.

Let's write detailed functions:
```
import numpy as np
import time

HARD_TIMEOUT = 2350.0

def next_pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    p=1
    while p<m: p <<=1
    return p

def normalize_sum(a, target=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if target is None:
        target = a.size
    S = a.sum()
    if S <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.shape, target/a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    out = a * (target / S)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    # clip may change sum slightly; renormalize again but preserve if possible
    S2=out.sum()
    if S2 <= 1e-15: return np.full(...)
    out = out * (target/S2)
    # if clip caused max>1000 after renorm? If out.max()>1000, maybe target/n >1000? n small? If n<target/1000? target=n, max if one element all mass >1000 for n<1? But typical n>=64 => max<=n=64 <1000. For n=1 target=1. safe. If n >1e6, maybe not. Not.
    return out

def fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0: return inf
    S=a.sum()
    if S<0.01: return inf
    c=fft_self_conv(a)
    M=c.max()
    if not np.isfinite(M): return inf
    return float(2.0*a.size*M/(S*S))
```
We can optimize fast_score using precomputed size? okay.

       h = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
       h = None
    if h is None: return np.ones(512)
    a=np.asarray(h,float)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    if a.size<16 or a.sum()<0.01: return np.ones(512)
    return a
```

`interpolate_mass(a, n)`:
```
def interpolate_mass(a, n):
    a=normalize_sum(a, a.size)
    p=a/a.size
    if n==a.size: return a.copy()
    x0=np.arange(a.size+1, dtype=float)/a.size
    c0=np.empty(a.size+1); c0[0]=0; c0[1:]=np.cumsum(p)
    c0[0]=0; c0[-1]=1
    x1=np.arange(n+1, dtype=float)/n
    c1=np.interp(x1, x0, c0)
    c1[0]=0; c1[-1]=1
    m=np.diff(c1)
    m=np.maximum(m,0)
    S=m.sum()
    if S<1e-15: return np.full(n,1.0)
    return m/n? Wait want sum n: m fractions sum1; a=m*n.
```
Return `m * n`. Good.

def top_lags(c, count, min_frac=0.0):
   thr = c.max()*min_frac
   idx=np.where(c>=max(thr, -np.inf))[0]
   idx=idx[np.argsort(-c[idx])]
   # ensure distinct? For LP, duplicate lags okay but waste; take unique.
   uniq=[]
   for i in idx:
       if len(uniq)>=count: break
       if all(abs(i-u)>1 for u in uniq[-?])? Too costly; just unique via np.unique.
   return list(idx[:count])
```

**Smooth step function**:
We'll implement a class-like via dict state. Since no closures, top-level function. State keys: `eta`, `tau`, `g_ema`, `iter`, `no_improve`, `scale`? No class.
```
def smooth_step(a, state, exact_best=None, global_best=None):
    n=a.size
    c=fft_self_conv(a)
    S=a.sum()
    M=c.max()
    score=2*n*M/(S*S) if S>0 else inf
    if not finite: normalize and return a,score,True,state? 
    # weights
    if M>0:
       x = c / M
       # c may have negative; x max 1
       x = np.clip(x, -10, 1) # avoid huge negative? x negative small.
       x -= x.max()
       # if tau not finite: tau=50
       w = np.exp(state['tau'] * x)
       wsum = w.sum()
       if not finite or wsum<=0:
          w = np.full_like(c, 1.0/(2*n-1))
       else: w /= wsum
    else: w uniform
    g = grad_correlate_weights(w, a)
    g = np.maximum(g,0)
    # EMA
    gamma=0.6
    if state.get('g_ema') is None: state['g_ema']=g.copy()
    else: state['g_ema'] = gamma*state['g_ema'] + (1-gamma)*g
    g_use = state['g_ema']
    gmax = np.maximum(g_use.max(), 1e-300)
    gn = g_use / gmax
    # optionally cap
    gn = np.minimum(gn, 2.0)
    eta = state['eta']
    # candidate
    a_new = a * np.exp(-eta * gn)
    a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 1e-12)
    a_new = normalize_sum(a_new, n)
    cand_score = fast_score(a_new)
    # if no improve and eta large try smaller
    improved=False
    if cand_score < score - 1e-12:
        a_out=a_new; new_score=cand_score; improved=True
        state['no_improve']=0
        state['eta']=min(max(eta*1.08, 1e-4), 0.7)
        state['tau']=state.get('tau',20.0)
    else:
        # try eta/2, /4
        for mult in (0.5,0.25):
            ae=a*np.exp(-eta*mult*gn); ...
        if best: accept and set eta=eta*mult*1.05
        else: state['eta']=max(eta*0.7,1e-4); state['no_improve']+=1
    # periodic increase tau
    if state['no_improve']>30:
       state['tau']=min(state['tau']*1.1, 800)
    if state['no_improve']>150:
       # random reset around normalized best? But this function doesn't know best. Could return signal.
    state['iter']+=1
    return a_out, new_score, improved, state
```
But if score maybe not best; outer tracks best. If candidate equal/no improve due to FFT precision, maybe accept tiny? Use threshold `score - 1e-10`.

**LP step function**:
```
def lp_step(a, state, limit_s=None):
   n=a.size
   p=a/a.sum()
   c_p=fft_self_conv(p)
   M=c_p.max()
   current_score=2*n*M
   active = top_lags(c_p, min(50, 2*n-1), min_frac=0.85) # top even if not all near max
   if len(active)<max(20, min(50...)): top 50
   q=p.copy()
   max_row=120
   improved_any=False
   start=time.monotonic()
   it=0
   while limit_s is None or time.monotonic()-start < limit_s:
       z = solve_zero_sum_lp(p, active, max_row)
       if z is None: break
       q=z[1:]; t=z[0]
       if q.sum() <=0: break
       q = q/q.sum()
       v = fft_conv(p,q)
       v = np.maximum(v,0)
       vmax = v.max()
       if not finite: break
       # Candidate line search
       q_a = q*n # sum n if a sum n current? a may sum n.
       alphas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7)
       best_alpha=None; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=fast_score(a) # or current_score converted? a score fast = 2nM/S^2 =2nM since sum=n; current_score.
       for alpha in alphas:
           cand=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*q_a
           cand=normalize_sum(cand,n)
           sc=fast_score(cand)
           if sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
               best_sc=sc; best_a=cand; best_alpha=alpha
       if best_a is not a:
           # check exact? Maybe not each time; return true and new a
           a=best_a
           improved_any=True
           # after acceptance, update p and maybe break or continue limited
           if it > 2 or (limit_s and time_left<2): break
           p=a/a.sum(); c_p=fft_self_conv(p); M=c_p.max()
           # active top maybe keep union with new
       else:
           # update active with violated
           # ifimax-vmax small: break
           if vmax - max(t,0) < 1e-11 * max(1.0, M):
               break
           viol = np.where(v - max(t,0) > 1e-11 * max(1.0, M))[0]
           if len(viol)==0: break
           new_active = list(viol[np.argsort(-v[viol])][:max_row])
           # union with previous top of c_p?
           old_active_set = set(active)
           for k in new_active:
               if k not in old_active_set and len(active)<max_row: active.append(k)
           # if active unchanged, maybe increase max_row or break
           if set(new_active).issubset(set(active)) and len(set(active) | set(new_active)) <= len(active):
               if it >= 5: break
       it+=1
   return a, improved_any
```

c_aq = fft_conv(a, q_a)
def score_alpha(alpha): return 2*n*max(combined)/n^2
```

`solve_zero_sum_lp(p, active, max_row)`:
```
def solve_zero_sum_lp(p, active, max_row):
    if not HAS_LP: return None
    n=p.size
    m=min(len(active), max_row)
    if m<=0: return np.concatenate([[1.0/n?], np.full(n,1/n)])? t maybe max? But no.
    # If m small, build A_ub (m,n+1)
    A = np.empty((m, n+1), dtype=np.float64)
    A[:,0] = -1.0
    A[:,1:] = 0.0
    for row,k in enumerate(active[:m]):
       start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)
       if end>=start:
          js=np.arange(start,end+1)
          d=k-js
          A[row, 1+start:1+end+1] = p[d]
    c_obj = np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[0]=1.0
    A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,1:] =1.0
    b_eq=np.array([1.0])
    bounds=[(0.0,None)] + [(0.0,None)]*n  # t lower 0
    # Optional: impose t≤M*2? Since p feasible with t=M; q with sum1 could have max cross maybe up to max p? To keep bounded, set t upper 1.0? Since p sum1, max p*q ≤ max p ≤1. Use upper 2.0. But if optimal t maybe >? p*q for q any prob ≤ max p (because each (p*q)_k sum p_i q_{k-i} ≤ max p * sum q_prob? if p_i≤maxp, weighted sum≤maxp; maxp can >1 if p nonconv? p sum1, maxp≤1. So t≤1. Good. Use t upper 1.0 to help. But if due to numerical, feasible t near M <1. So bounds t (0,1.0) or (0,None). Use (0,1.0+1e-9).
    res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.zeros(m), A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    if res is None or not res.success: return None
    z=res.x
    if not finite: return None
    q=z[1:]; q=np.maximum(q,0)
    if q.sum()<1e-12: return None
    q/=q.sum()
    t=float(max(z[0], 0.0))
    return np.concatenate(([t], q))
```
Need ensure active include constraints from previous; A_ub for each row as `c - t ≤0`. We set A[:,0]=-1, A[:,1:]=coeffs; b=0. Good.
If m=0, no constraints; min t with sum q=1 and q≥0 => t=0 (unbounded? t≥0, q anything). q could arbitrary; not direction. But active nonempty.
Potential issue: Linprog with variable t upper 1.0. If the selected constraints force t >1? impossible because p sum1, max(p*q)≤max p ≤1. But if p has values >1? p sum1 nonnegative, max≤1. Good.

**Potential bug**: In smooth gradient, `grad_i = correlation(w,a)`. For c_k = sum_j a_j a_{k-j}, derivative wrt a_i = sum_k w_k ∂c_k = sum_k w_k [a_{k-i} + a_{?}]? Let's derive using product: c_k = ∑_{j} a_j a_{k-j}. ∂/∂a_i = ∑_j [δ_{j,i} a_{k-j} + a_j δ_{k-j,i}] = ∑_j (δ_{j,i} + δ_{j,k-i}) a_{k-j} = a_{k-i} (if valid) + a_{k-(k-i)}=a_i (if k-i valid). **Fast score formula for sum n**: If a sum n, S=n, score=2n*M/n^2=2M/n. But our fast_score uses general formula. For candidate α from a and q_a both sum n, c_alpha as combination of convs; max. Implement `score_from_convs(c_alpha, n)`:
`return 2.0*n*float(np.max(c_alpha))/(n*n)`. Since candidate sum n. If due to numerical sum not exactly n, can compute actual sum? If candidate after bilinear not normalized. But a and q_a normalized sum n, linear combination exact sum n (thanks floating maybe). Use formula. For safety, after line search we could normalize candidate and use fast_score once. But for choosing alpha, bilinear enough.

In LP line search, we need c_q and c_aq. Compute with `fft_self_conv(q_a)` and `fft_conv(a,q_a)`. Then for alpha:
```
c = (1-alpha)*(1-alpha)*c_a + 2*alpha*(1-alpha)*c_aq + alpha*alpha*c_q
M = c.max()
sc = 2*n*M/(n*n) # because sum n
```
If alpha small, c close. Choose min.

**Potential exact/FFT mismatch**: Bilinear combination of FFT convs approximates FFT of combined due to linearity, but c_a etc from separate FFTs with same length. Should be consistent. Good.

`top_lags`:
```
def top_lags(c, count, min_frac=0.0):
    if c.size==0: return []
    cm = float(np.max(c))
    if cm <=0: return []
    idx = np.where(c >= cm*min_frac)[0]
    if idx.size==0: idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
    # sort descending by value
    if idx.size > count*3:
       order = np.argsort(-c[idx])[:count*3]
       idx = idx[order]
    else:
       idx = idx[np.argsort(-c[idx])]
    idx = np.unique(idx)  # sorts asc
    # But we want top by c; unique may reorder. Instead after unique, sort by c desc.
    idx = idx[np.argsort(-c[idx])]
    return [int(i) for i in idx[:count]]
```
If min_frac=0.9, many. If min_frac=0, all. For initial active, top 80 with min_frac=0? top 80. Good.

The min t won't exceed M for q=p, M<=? p*p max <= max p? p sum1 => any (p*p)_k =∑p_i p_{k-i} ≤ max p_i ∑_{valid} p_{k-i} ≤ max p *1 ≤1. So upper 1+1e-9 sufficient. Use `10.0` not too large; still bounded. Bigger upper may make LP unbounded? no. Use 10.

Function `phase_search(a, limit_s, global_state)`:
```
def phase_search(a, limit_s, global_state):
   n=a.size; deadline=time+limit_s
   L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
   p=a/a.sum(); p_full=zeros L; p_full[:n]=p
   P=rfft(p_full,L)
   a_cur=normalize_sum(a,n); sc_cur=fast_score(a_cur)
   improved=False
   factors=[0.99,0.98,0.97,0.96]
   for factor in factors:
      if time>deadline: break
      for it in range(10):
         if time>deadline: break
         # current P from a_cur? Each iteration from P? Better update P each time.
         P = rfft(pad a_cur)
         c = irfft(P*P,L)[:2n-1].real; c=np.maximum(c,0); sc=2n*max(c)/S^2 (S=n)
         M=c.max(); cap=M*factor; if cap<=0 break
         c_new=c.copy(); m=c_new>cap; excess=c_new[m].sum-cap;m.sum
         c_new[m]=cap
         head=cap-c_new
         if excess>0 and head.sum>0: c_new += excess*head/head.sum()
         c_new=np.maximum(c_new,0); c_new/=c_new.sum()
         C=np.fft.rfft(c_new,L)
         mag=np.sqrt(np.abs(C))
         phase = P / np.maximum(np.abs(P), 1e-300)
         P_new=mag*phase
         q_full=np.fft.irfft(P_new,L)
         q=q_full[:n]
         q=np.maximum(q,0); qsum=q.sum()
         if qsum<1e-12: break
         q = q/qsum*n
         # line search alphas [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8]
         best_sc=sc_cur; best_a=a_cur
         for alpha in ...:
            cand=(1-alpha)*a_cur+alpha*q; normalize; sc=fast_score(cand); update
         if best_sc < sc_cur - 1e-10:
            a_cur=best_a; sc_cur=best_sc; improved=True; update global
         else:
            # maybe break this factor
            break
   return a_cur, improved
```
This is a lot; but can act as stagnation break. Need ensure P phase for P=0. Use `phase = P / np.abs(P)`; abs zero -> 1. With rfft, P complex.
Potential issue: In `C=np.fft.rfft(c_new,L)`, c_new length 2n-1 but for irfft of P^2 to produce c, P should be length L with zero pad. The DFT of c_new zero-padded to L equals P^2 if c_new corresponds to linear convolution. We set c_new length 2n-1; rfft pads to L. Good. But c_new not zero-padded to L? rfft pads. Good.
The `mag` from |C| can be large near m=0? C[0]=sum c_new=1, mag[0]=1. P[0]=sum a=n? Wait a sum n, P[0]=sum p_full =1 if p. If using a not p, P(0)=n. Need be consistent. In phase_search, use p to make P(0)=1. q returned sum1; q_a=q*n for candidate. Good.
In c calculation: if p sum1, c conv length values ~1/n; M~0.0003. cap etc. score =2n*M. `c_new` sum1.
Construct P from p each iteration. Good.

Maybe phase_search not reliable; but if it improves, okay.

```
m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*gn
v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*gn**2

loga = log(a + floor) - step
a = exp(loga - max); normalize
```

      start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)
      if end>=start:
          js=np.arange(start,end+1)
          d=k-js
          g[start:end+1] += w*a[d]
   # To account for omitted small weights? We normalized weights sum1; if sum omitted small, ok approx. Could scale g by 1/sum_act to preserve gradient magnitude.
   s=weights[act].sum()
   if s>0: g /= s
else:
   g=grad_correlate_weights(weights,a)
```
If we scale by sum_act, gradient magnitude consistent with total weight (omitted negligible). Good. This direct method uses top significant weights. It may be faster than FFT.

```

`next_pow2` as while.

`normalize_sum`:
```
def normalize_sum(a, target=None):
    if target is None: target = SUM_TARGET
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
       return np.array([1.0])
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-15:
       return np.full(a.size, target/a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    out = a * (target / S)
    # No need clip to 1000 because target=500, but if target >1000? maybe; use clip.
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S2=float(out.sum())
    if S2 <=1e-15:
       return np.full(a.size, target/a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    out = out * (target/S2)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return out
```
If target/S causes max>1000? target 500, no. If out clipped and renormalized, max might exceed? Suppose out one val >1000 clipped to1000, sum maybe <target, renormalize to target could make it 1000*target/sum >1000 if other vals clipped? But target=500<1000, so even if sum after clip <500, renormalized one val could >1000? Example one val original huge -> scaled to max maybe? Since out before clip is a*target/S, max could be target if one element all mass =500; no clip. So clip no effect. Good.

`fft_self_conv`:
```
def fft_self_conv(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n==1: return a*a
    size=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
Maybe `irfft` returns length size; due to numerical, c beyond 2n-1 small. Good.

`fft_conv` similarly.

`grad_correlate_weights`:
```
def grad_correlate_weights(w, a):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n==1: return a[0]*w[0] np array?
    size=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,size)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], size)
    g=np.fft.irfft(W*A, size)
    return g[n-1:2*n-1]
```
For n=1, `a[::-1]` length1, size next_pow2(1)=1, g[0:1] fine. No special.

`compute_grad_smooth` direct/FFT:
```
def compute_grad_smooth(w, a):
    n=len(a)
    act = np.where(w > 1e-4)[0]
    if act.size > 0 and act.size <= 60:
        g=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float64)
        s=0.0
        for ii in range(act.size):
            k=int(act[ii]); wt=float(w[k])
            if wt<=0: continue
            start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)
            if end>=start:
                js=np.arange(start,end+1,dtype=np.int64)
                d=k-js
                g[start:end+1] += wt * a[d]
                s += wt
        if s > 1e-15:
            g /= s
        else:
            return grad_correlate_weights(w,a)
        return g
    else:
        return grad_correlate_weights(w,a)
```
This direct gradient omits weights below 1e-4, scale by sum. If act empty, all weights negligible? not.
Could thresholds cause grad zero if w all low? For uniform weights over 2n (4000), individual 0.00025 >1e-4 for n<5000, act size 4000>60 -> FFT. For n8192, uniform weight 6e-5<1e-4, act empty -> FFT. Good.

`fast_score`:
```
def fast_score(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   if a.size==0: return inf
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
   S=a.sum()
   if S<0.01: return inf
   c=fft_self_conv(a)
   if c.size==0: return inf
   M=float(c.max())
   if not np.isfinite(M): return inf
   return float(2.0*a.size*M/(S*S))
```
We don't clip negatives, but sum may include negative if a raw. We should `a=np.maximum(a,0)`? Evaluator clips. For fast candidate from normalize nonneg; but for initial maybe. Use `a=np.maximum(a,0)` to match. It might change score if input had tiny negative duplicate; okay.

def exact_score_array(a):
    try:
       lst=[float(x) for x in np.asarray(a)]
       val=evaluate_sequence(lst)
       val=float(val)
       if not np.isfinite(val): return inf
       return val
    except Exception:
       return inf
```

def get_parent():
    h = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    if h is None:
       return np.ones(1024, dtype=float)
    try:
       a=np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
       return np.ones(1024)
    if a.ndim !=1:
       a=a.ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    if a.size<16 or a.sum()<0.01:
       return np.ones(1024)
    # if length huge >16384? We may downsample to target later; but get parent as is.
    return a
```

`interpolate_mass`:
```
def interpolate_mass(a, n):
    a=normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    if n == a.size: return a.copy()
    S0=SUM_TARGET
    p=a/S0
    n0=a.size
    x0=np.arange(n0+1,dtype=float)/n0
    c0=np.empty(n0+1,float); c0[0]=0; c0[1:]=np.cumsum(p); c0[0]=0; c0[-1]=1.0
    x1=np.arange(n+1,dtype=float)/n
    c1=np.interp(x1,x0,c0)
    c1[0]=0; c1[-1]=1
    m=np.diff(c1)
    m=np.maximum(m,0)
    ms=m.sum()
    if ms<1e-15: return np.full(n,S0/n)
    m *= S0/ms
    return m
```
If n huge and S0/n small, okay.

`fast candidates` maybe also direct parent at length n0 and upsample target. If parent n0 >8192, maybe downsample to 8192? We'll generate `down = interpolate_mass(parent, 8192)`.

`top_lags` as above.

`solve_minmax_lp` top-level. Need define MAX_ACTIVE maybe inside.
```
def solve_minmax_lp(p, active, max_active):
    if not HAS_LP: return None
    n=int(p.size)
    if n<=0: return None
    if len(active)==0: return None
    m=0
    A=[] maybe prealloc
```
Prealloc dense:
```
    A = np.zeros((max_active, n+1), float)
    rows=0
    for k in active:
       if rows >= max_active: break
       k=int(k)
       if k<0 or k>=2*n-1: continue
       A[rows,0] = -1.0
       start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)
       if end>=start:
          js=np.arange(start,end+1)
          d=k-js
          A[rows, 1+start:1+end+1] = p[d]
       rows+=1
    if rows==0: return None
    A=A[:rows]
    b=np.zeros(rows)
    cobj=np.zeros(n+1); cobj[0]=1
    Aeq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); Aeq[0,1:]=1
    beq=np.array([1.0])
    bounds=[(0.0, 10.0)] + [(0.0, None)]*n
    try:
       res=linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=beq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or not res.success: return None
    z=res.x
    if z.size != n+1: return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)): return None
    q=z[1:]; q=np.maximum(q,0)
    qsum=q.sum()
    if qsum <= 1e-12: return None
    q /= qsum
    t=float(max(0.0, z[0]))
    return np.concatenate(([t], q))
```
Potential memory if max_active=150,n=8192: A 150*8193=1.2M. Good. If n=16384, 2.46M 19MB. okay.
`rows` active could have duplicate lags? We pass unique. If duplicate, duplicate constraints waste. top_lags unique.

`lp_step` with global update:
Need use `fast_score`? We'll compute line search from convs.
```
def lp_step(a, limit_s, global_state):
    a=normalize_sum(a)
    n=int(a.size)
    S=float(a.sum())
    start=time.monotonic()
    improved=False
    prev_active=None
    for it in range(8):
        if time.monotonic() - start > limit_s: break
        p=a/S
        c_p=fft_self_conv(p)
        M=float(c_p.max())
        if M <=0: break
        if prev_active is None:
             active=top_lags(c_p, 80, 0.0)
        # solve
        z=solve_minmax_lp(p, active, 150)
        if z is None: break
        t=float(z[0]); q=z[1:]; qsum=q.sum()
        if qsum<=1e-12: break
        q=q/qsum
        q_a=q*S
        c_a=fft_self_conv(a)
        c_q=fft_self_conv(q_a)
        c_aq=fft_conv(a,q_a)
        best_sc=2.0*n*float(c_a.max())/(S*S) # current
        best_a=a; best_alpha=0.0
        alphas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.4,0.6,0.8)
        for alpha in alphas:
             if alpha>=1.0: continue
             c = (1-alpha)*(1-alpha)*c_a + 2.0*alpha*(1-alpha)*c_aq + alpha*alpha*c_q
             M2=float(c.max())
             sc=2.0*n*M2/(S*S)
             if sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
                 best_sc=sc; best_alpha=alpha
                 best_a=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*q_a
        if best_alpha > 0:
             best_a=normalize_sum(best_a, S)
             # update global fast
             update_global(global_state, best_a, 'lp')
             a=best_a; improved=True
             # after first accept, we can do one or two more if time
             if time.monotonic() - start > limit_s * 0.7 or it >= 4:
                 break
             # set prev_active to top of new c_p? recompute next loop; maybe keep some active? We'll recompute.
             prev_active=None
             continue
        else:
             v=fft_conv(p,q)
             v=np.maximum(v,0)
             vmax=float(v.max())
             tol=1e-10*max(1.0, M)
             if vmax <= t + tol:
                 break
             viol=np.where(v > t + tol)[0]
             if viol.size ==0: break
             vidx=viol[np.argsort(-v[viol])][:150]
             merged = np.unique(np.concatenate((np.array(active, dtype=int), np.array(vidx, dtype=int))))
             merged = merged[np.argsort(-v[merged])]
             new_active=[int(x) for x in merged[:150]]
             if prev_active is not None and new_active == prev_active:
                 break
             active=new_active
             prev_active=new_active
    return a, improved
```

`update_global` function:
```
def update_global(gstate, a, tag=''):
    a=normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    sc=fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): return
    if sc < gstate['best_score'] - 1e-12:
       gstate['best_score']=sc
       gstate['best_a']=a.copy()
       gstate['best_tag']=tag
    # pool
    pool=gstate['pool']
    # if sc good, replace worst
    if sc <= gstate['best_score'] + 0.02:
       # if pool length <3 or sc < worst score
       if len(pool) < 3:
           pool.append((sc,a.copy()))
       else:
           worst=pool[0][0]
           for i in range(len(pool)):
               if pool[i][0] > worst:
                   worst=pool[i][0]; wi=i
           if sc < worst - 1e-9:
               pool[wi]=(sc,a.copy())
```
Need initialize pool as list. If multiple tags same, okay.
No lambda.

def smooth_step(a, state, global_state):
    a=normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    S=float(a.sum())
    sc=fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
       # restore from global best if possible
       if 'best_a' in global_state and global_state['best_a'] is not None:
           a=global_state['best_a'].copy()
       else:
           a=np.full(a.size, SUM_TARGET/a.size)
       sc=fast_score(a)
    update_global(global_state, a, 'smooth')
    n=a.size
    c=fft_self_conv(a)
    M=float(c.max())
    if M <=0:
       return a, sc, True, state
    x=(c - M)/(M + 1e-15)
    x=np.minimum(x, 0.0)
    # if x range extremely small, make uniform? if max x - min x < 1e-8, w uniform
    x_range=np.max(x)-np.min(x)
    if x_range < 1e-8:
        w=np.full(c.size, 1.0/c.size)
    else:
        w=np.exp(state.get('tau',30.0)*x)
        wsum=w.sum()
        if not np.isfinite(wsum) or wsum <=0:
            w=np.full(c.size,1.0/c.size)
        else:
            w=w/wsum
    g=compute_grad_smooth(w,a)
    g=np.maximum(g,0)
    # EMA
    ema=state.get('g_ema')
    if ema is None or ema.size != n:
        state['g_ema']=g.copy()
    else:
        state['g_ema']=0.7*ema + 0.3*g
    g=state['g_ema']
    gmax=float(g.max())
    if gmax <= 1e-300:
        return a, sc, False, state
    gn=g/gmax
    gn=np.minimum(gn, 2.0)
    eta=float(state.get('eta', 0.08))
    floor=1e-10 * SUM_TARGET / max(1,n)
    improved=False
    def candidate(eta_val): # nested function not allowed? Rule no closures? Better not define nested.
```

`random_perturb`:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    a=normalize_sum(a)
    noise=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma,a.size))
    cand=a*noise
    cand=np.maximum(cand,0)
    cand=normalize_sum(cand, SUM_TARGET)
    return cand
```

`phase_search` top-level. Need no nested. Could be simpler and less critical. Let's implement robustly with explicit loops.
```
def phase_search(a, limit_s, global_state):
    a=normalize_sum(a)
    n=a.size
    S=SUM_TARGET
    deadline=time.monotonic()+limit_s
    L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    improved=False
    factors=(0.99,0.98,0.97,0.96)
    for factor in factors:
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       for _ in range(8):
          if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
          p=a/S
          pad=np.zeros(L); pad[:n]=p
          P=np.fft.rfft(pad, L)
          c=np.fft.irfft(P*P, L)[:2*n-1]
          c=np.maximum(c,0)
          M=float(c.max())
          if M<=0: break
          cap=M*factor
          c_new=c.copy()
          mask=c_new>cap
          excess=float(c_new[mask].sum()) - cap*float(mask.sum())
          if excess <= 0:
              break
          c_new[mask]=cap
          head=cap - c_new
          headsum=float(head.sum())
          if headsum <= 1e-15:
              c_new = c_new / max(1e-15, c_new.sum())
          else:
              c_new += (excess/headsum) * head
              c_new=np.maximum(c_new,0)
              snew=float(c_new.sum())
              if snew <=1e-15: c_new=c/max(1e-15,c.max());?
              else: c_new/=snew
          C=np.fft.rfft(c_new, L)
          mag=np.sqrt(np.maximum(np.abs(C), 0.0))
          Ap=np.abs(P)
          phase=np.ones_like(P, dtype=complex)
          maskp=Ap>1e-300
          phase[maskp]=P[maskp]/Ap[maskp]
          Pn=mag*phase
          q_full=np.fft.irfft(Pn, L)
          q=q_full[:n]
          q=np.maximum(q,0)
          qsum=float(q.sum())
          if qsum <=1e-12: break
          q=q/qsum*S
          best_sc=fast_score(a)
          best_a=a
          for alpha in (0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8):
              cand=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*q
              cand=normalize_sum(cand,S)
              sc=fast_score(cand)
              if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                  best_sc=sc; best_a=cand
          if best_a is not a:
              a=best_a; improved=True; update_global(global_state,a,'phase')
          else:
              break
    return a, improved
```
This uses complex phase; rfft returns complex array. `np.ones_like(P, dtype=complex)` okay. `phase[maskp]` works. For n large, phase_search a few iterations. It may be expensive but okay. Note: c_new length 2n-1, C=rfft pads to L; C[0]=1. mag[0]=1. Phase from P[0]=1.
Potential bug: If P has zeros, mag*phase with phase1 may yield values not Hermitian? rfft handles. Good.
Could improve by reusing P? okay.

**optimize_candidate** full:
```
def optimize_candidate(a, budget, global_state, seed):
    a=normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    n=a.size
    state={'eta':0.08, 'tau':30.0, 'no_improve':0, 'iter':0, 'g_ema':None}
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget
    reserve=60.0 if not HAS_LP else 120.0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    last_improve=time.monotonic()
    next_lp=time.monotonic()+120.0
    next_phase=time.monotonic()+600.0
    last_reset=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() + reserve < deadline:
        now=time.monotonic()
        stagnant = now - last_improve
        if HAS_LP and now > next_lp and time.monotonic() + reserve + 60 < deadline:
            a, imp = lp_step(a, min(90.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - reserve - 10), global_state)
            next_lp = time.monotonic() + 240.0
            if imp:
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                state['no_improve']=0
                state['eta']=min(0.1, max(0.05, state['eta']*1.1))
                state['tau']=min(200.0, state.get('tau',30.0)*0.8)
        elif stagnant > 600 and now > next_phase and time.monotonic() + reserve + 30 < deadline:
            a, imp = phase_search(a, min(60.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - reserve - 10), global_state)
            next_phase = time.monotonic() + 900.0
            if imp:
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                state['no_improve']=0
            else:
                # random restart from global best or current best
                if global_state.get('best_a') is not None and global_state['best_a'].size == n:
                    base = global_state['best_a'].copy()
                else:
                    base = a.copy()
                sigma = 0.15 if len([r for r in (base>0).tolist() if r])>10 else 0.3
                for sigma_try in (0.1,0.2,0.4):
                    cand=random_perturb(base, rng, sigma_try)
                    if fast_score(cand) < fast_score(a)-1e-6:
                         a=cand; break
                # maybe reset state
                state['eta']=0.08; state['tau']=30.0; state['no_improve']=0; state['g_ema']=None
                last_reset=time.monotonic()
        else:
            a, sc, imp, state = smooth_step(a, state, global_state)
            if imp:
                last_improve=time.monotonic()
                if time.monotonic() - last_reset > 120:
                    # don't reset again soon
                    pass
    # final polish
    # If global best at same length and better than current a? Use global best?
    if global_state.get('best_a') is not None and global_state['best_a'].size == n:
        a = global_state['best_a'].copy()
    else: a=normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    state['eta']=0.005
    state['tau']=max(20.0, min(200.0, state.get('tau',30.0)*0.5))
    state['no_improve']=0
    while time.monotonic() + 2.0 < deadline:
        a, sc, imp, state = smooth_step(a, state, global_state)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            break
    return a
```

`prepare_final`:
```
def prepare_final(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    if a.size==0: a=np.ones(1024)
    S=a.sum()
    if S<=1e-12:
       a=np.full(a.size, SUM_TARGET/a.size)
    else:
       a=a*(SUM_TARGET/S)
    mx=a.max()
    if mx>1000.0:
       a=a*(1000.0/mx)
    # Scale invariant but sum may be less than0.01 if n huge? If mx>1000 and one element, after scale sum=1000>0.01.
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    return a
```
If after scaling max maybe exactly 1000. Good.

Let's implement LP functions carefully to avoid errors.

`solve_maxsum_lp`:
```
def solve_maxsum_lp(p, active, M, U, max_active):
  if not HAS_LP: return None
  n=p.size
  A=np.zeros((max_active,n),float)
  rows=0
  for k in active:
    if rows>=max_active: break
    ... A[rows, start:end+1]=p[d]
    rows+=1
  if rows==0: return None
  A=A[:rows]
  cobj=-np.ones(n)
  b=M*np.ones(rows)
  bounds=[(0.0,U)]*n
  res=linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
  q=res.x; q=np.maximum(q,0); s=q.sum(); if s<=1e-12 return None
  return q
```
No equality. If maxsum LP fails due to presolve with bounds, okay.

`lp_step_maxsum`:
```
def lp_step_maxsum(a, limit_s, gstate):
  a=normalize_sum(a,S)
  n=a.size; S=float(a.sum())
  start=time.monotonic()
  improved=False
  active=None
  for it in range(5):
    if time - start > limit_s: break
    p=a/S; c_p=fft_self_conv(p); M=c_p.max()
    if active is None: active=top_lags(c_p,80)
    maxp=float(np.max(p))
    U=max(20.0/n, 10.0*maxp, 0.02, maxp*1.1)
    q=solve_maxsum_lp(p, active, M, U, 150)
    if q is None: break
    qsum=q.sum()
    if qsum <=1e-12: break
    qn=q/qsum*S
    # line search
    c_a=fft_self_conv(a); c_q=fft_self_conv(qn); c_aq=fft_conv(a,qn)
    best_sc=...; for alpha ...
    if best_alpha>0: accept, update global, return a, True
    else:
      v=fft_conv(p, q) # Note q not normalized? The LP constraints used q unnormalized. p*q same.
      v=np.maximum(v,0)
      viol=np.where(v > M + 1e-10*max(1,M))[0]
      if viol.size==0: break
      vidx=viol[np.argsort(-v[viol])][:150]
      merged=np.unique(np.concatenate(...)); sort by v; active=...
      if active same: break
  return a, False
```
Should use q normalized for line search, but v constraints use q unnormalized. Good.
If qsum very large, qn same direction. If qsum ≤1, direction q may be p; no improve.

def make_parametric(n):
    shapes=[]
    u=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    # use (u + 1e-8) for sqrt
    shapes.append(np.ones(n))
    shapes.append(1.0 + 0.4*u)
    shapes.append(1.0 - 0.4*u)
    shapes.append(np.sqrt(u+1e-8) + np.sqrt(1.0-u+1e-8))
    shapes.append(np.exp(-8.0*(u-0.5)*(u-0.5)))
    shapes.append(0.5 + 0.5*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*u))
    shapes.append(0.5 + 0.5*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*u + 0.5))
    shapes.append(1.0 + 0.3*np.sin(4.0*np.pi*u))
    out=[]
    for s in shapes:
       a=np.maximum(s,0)
       a=normalize_sum(a,SUM_TARGET)
       out.append(a)
    return out
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-height vector that minimizes

    2 * n * max(np.convolve(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

The code uses:
1. Mass-preserving interpolation of the previous parent to several lengths.
2. A different history of fast candidates, including parametric starts.
3. A mirror/multiplicative smooth subgradient descent on a log-sum-exp surrogate.
4. Convex LP directions (cutting-plane zero-sum min-max directions and
   SOTA-style max-sum directions) solved with HiGHS when available.
5. A cautious spectral / phase-retrieval restart for escape from local minima.
6. Periodic final exact validation with evaluate_sequence().

Only top-level helpers are used. No lambdas. No filesystem/network.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAS_LP = True
except Exception:
    HAS_LP = False

# The objective is scale invariant.  We optimize at a fixed moderate sum so that
# all values are far below the 1000 clipping bound.
SUM_TARGET = 500.0
MAX_LEN = 8192


def next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    p = 1
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def normalize_sum(a, target=None):
    if target is None:
        target = SUM_TARGET
    target = float(target)
    a = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.size, target / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    out = a * (target / s)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(out.sum())
    if s2 <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.size, target / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    out = out * (target / s2)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return out


def fft_self_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return a * a
    size = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(c[:2 * n - 1])


def fft_conv(a, b):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    m = int(b.size)
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = next_pow2(n + m - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, size)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(c[:n + m - 1])


def grad_correlate_weights(w, a):
    """
    For weights w of length 2n-1 and a of length n, return
    g_i = sum_d w[i+d] a[d], i=0..n-1.
    This is the unnormalized gradient contribution of sum_k w_k c_k.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, size)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], size)
    g = np.fft.irfft(fw * fa, size)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(g[n - 1:2 * n - 1])


def compute_grad_smooth(w, a):
    """
    Compute the smooth surrogate gradient.  For very concentrated weight vectors,
    a direct active-peak sum is faster than FFT correlation.
    """
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    act = np.where(w > 1e-4)[0]
    if act.size > 0 and act.size <= 60:
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = 0.0
        for ii in range(act.size):
            k = int(act[ii])
            wt = float(w[k])
            if wt <= 0.0:
                continue
            start = max(0, k - n + 1)
            end = min(n - 1, k)
            if end >= start:
                js = np.arange(start, end + 1, dtype=np.int64)
                d = k - js
                g[start:end + 1] += wt * a[d]
                s += wt
        if s > 0.5:
            g /= s
            return g
    return grad_correlate_weights(w, a)


def fast_score(a):
    a = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    n = int(a.size)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    c = fft_self_conv(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def exact_score_array(a):
    evalfn = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
    if evalfn is None:
        return float("inf")
    try:
        a = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)).ravel()
        lst = [float(x) for x in a]
        v = float(evalfn(lst))
        if not np.isfinite(v):
            return float("inf")
        return v
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def get_parent():
    h = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    if h is None:
        return np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
    try:
        a = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if a.size < 16 or float(a.sum()) < 0.01:
        return np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
    return a


def interpolate_mass(a, n):
    """Mass-preserving interpolation of a step distribution to length n."""
    a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    n0 = int(a.size)
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == n0:
        return a.copy()
    s0 = float(a.sum())
    if s0 <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(n, SUM_TARGET / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    p = a / s0
    x0 = np.arange(n0 + 1, dtype=np.float64) / float(n0)
    c0 = np.empty(n0 + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    c0[0] = 0.0
    c0[1:] = np.cumsum(p)
    c0[-1] = 1.0
    x1 = np.arange(n + 1, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    c1 = np.interp(x1, x0, c0)
    c1[0] = 0.0
    c1[-1] = 1.0
    m = np.diff(c1)
    m = np.maximum(m, 0.0)
    ms = float(m.sum())
    if ms <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(n, SUM_TARGET / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    m = m * (SUM_TARGET / ms)
    return normalize_sum(m, SUM_TARGET)


def top_lags(c, count, min_frac=0.0):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size == 0:
        return []
    count = int(max(1, count))
    cm = float(np.max(c))
    if cm <= 0.0:
        return []
    idx = np.argsort(-c)[:min(c.size, max(count, 16))]
    idx = np.unique(idx)
    idx = idx[np.argsort(-c[idx])]
    out = []
    for ii in idx:
        if ii < 0 or ii >= c.size:
            continue
        if float(cm * min_frac) <= 1e-300 or float(c[ii]) >= cm * float(min_frac):
            out.append(int(ii))
        if len(out) >= count:
            break
    return out


def update_global(gstate, a, tag=""):
    a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    sc = fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return
    if gstate.get("best_score", float("inf")) is None:
        gstate["best_score"] = float("inf")
    if sc < float(gstate.get("best_score", float("inf"))) - 1e-12:
        gstate["best_score"] = float(sc)
        gstate["best_a"] = a.copy()
        gstate["best_tag"] = tag
    pool = gstate.get("pool")
    if pool is None:
        gstate["pool"] = []
        pool = gstate["pool"]
    if sc <= float(gstate.get("best_score", float("inf"))) + 0.03:
        if len(pool) < 3:
            pool.append((float(sc), a.copy()))
        else:
            worst_i = 0
            worst = float(pool[0][0])
            for i in range(1, len(pool)):
                if float(pool[i][0]) > worst:
                    worst = float(pool[i][0])
                    worst_i = i
            if sc < worst - 1e-9:
                pool[worst_i] = (float(sc), a.copy())


def solve_minmax_lp(p, active, max_active, time_limit_s):
    """
    Solve, over a selected active set, the min-max LP:
        minimize t
        s.t. (p*q)_k <= t for k in active, sum(q)=1, q>=0.
    Returns [t, q].
    """
    if not HAS_LP:
        return None
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0 or len(active) == 0:
        return None
    max_active = int(min(max_active, len(active), 150))
    A = np.zeros((max_active, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    rows = 0
    for kk in active:
        if rows >= max_active:
            break
        k = int(kk)
        if k < 0 or k >= 2 * n - 1:
            continue
        A[rows, 0] = -1.0
        start = max(0, k - n + 1)
        end = min(n - 1, k)
        if end >= start:
            js = np.arange(start, end + 1, dtype=np.int64)
            d = k - js
            A[rows, 1 + start:1 + end + 1] = p[d]
        rows += 1
    if rows == 0:
        return None
    A = A[:rows]
    b = np.zeros(rows, dtype=np.float64)
    cobj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cobj[0] = 1.0
    Aeq = np.zeros((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    Aeq[0, 1:] = 1.0
    beq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] + [(0.0, None)] * n
    try:
        opts = {"time_limit": float(time_limit_s)}
        res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=beq,
                      bounds=bounds, method="highs", options=opts)
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=beq,
                          bounds=bounds, method="highs")
        except Exception:
            return None
    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None
    z = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if z is None or z.size != n + 1 or not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        return None
    q = np.asarray(z[1:], dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    qs = float(q.sum())
    if qs <= 1e-12:
        return None
    q /= qs
    t = float(max(0.0, z[0]))
    return np.concatenate(([t], q))


def solve_maxsum_lp(p, active, M, U, max_active, time_limit_s):
    """
    Solve, over selected active constraints,
        maximize sum(q)
        s.t. (p*q)_k <= M for k in active, 0 <= q <= U.
    """
    if not HAS_LP:
        return None
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0 or len(active) == 0 or M <= 0.0:
        return None
    max_active = int(min(max_active, len(active), 150))
    A = np.zeros((max_active, n), dtype=np.float64)
    rows = 0
    for kk in active:
        if rows >= max_active:
            break
        k = int(kk)
        if k < 0 or k >= 2 * n - 1:
            continue
        start = max(0, k - n + 1)
        end = min(n - 1, k)
        if end >= start:
            js = np.arange(start, end + 1, dtype=np.int64)
            d = k - js
            A[rows, start:end + 1] = p[d]
        rows += 1
    if rows == 0:
        return None
    A = A[:rows]
    b = float(M) * np.ones(rows, dtype=np.float64)
    cobj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, float(U))] * n
    try:
        opts = {"time_limit": float(time_limit_s)}
        res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method="highs", options=opts)
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
        except Exception:
            return None
    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None
    q = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if q is None or q.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(q)):
        return None
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    if float(q.sum()) <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return q


def lp_line_search(a, direction_a):
    """Given current a and normalized direction_a, try several small steps."""
    a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    direction_a = normalize_sum(direction_a, SUM_TARGET)
    n = int(a.size)
    s = float(a.sum())
    ca = fft_self_conv(a)
    cq = fft_self_conv(direction_a)
    caq = fft_conv(a, direction_a)
    best_sc = float(2.0 * n * np.max(ca) / (s * s))
    best_a = a
    best_alpha = 0.0
    alphas = (0.01, 0.03, 0.08, 0.15, 0.25, 0.40, 0.65)
    for alpha in alphas:
        if alpha <= 0.0 or alpha >= 1.0:
            continue
        aa = 1.0 - alpha
        c = (aa * aa) * ca + (2.0 * alpha * aa) * caq + (alpha * alpha) * cq
        m = float(np.max(c))
        sc = float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
            best_sc = sc
            best_alpha = alpha
            best_a = aa * a + alpha * direction_a
    if best_alpha > 0.0:
        best_a = normalize_sum(best_a, SUM_TARGET)
    return best_a, best_sc, best_alpha


def lp_step_maxsum(a, limit_s, gstate):
    try:
        a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
        n = int(a.size)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if n == 0 or s <= 0:
            return a, False
        start = time.monotonic()
        active = None
        for _ in range(5):
            if time.monotonic() - start > limit_s:
                break
            p = a / s
            cp = fft_self_conv(p)
            M = float(np.max(cp))
            if M <= 0.0:
                break
            if active is None:
                active = top_lags(cp, 80, 0.0)
            maxp = float(np.max(p))
            U = max(20.0 / float(n), 10.0 * maxp, 0.02, 1.1 * maxp)
            remaining = max(5.0, limit_s - (time.monotonic() - start))
            solve_safe = min(35.0, remaining)
            q = solve_maxsum_lp(p, active, M, U, 150, solve_safe)
            if q is None:
                break
            qs = float(q.sum())
            if qs <= 1e-12:
                break
            qdir = (q / qs) * s
            best_a, best_sc, best_alpha = lp_line_search(a, qdir)
            if best_alpha > 0.0:
                update_global(gstate, best_a, "lp_maxsum")
                return best_a, True
            v = fft_conv(p, q)
            v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
            tol = 1e-10 * max(1.0, M)
            viol = np.where(v > M + tol)[0]
            if viol.size == 0:
                break
            vtop = viol[np.argsort(-v[viol])][:150]
            merged = np.unique(np.concatenate((np.asarray(active, dtype=np.int64),
                                               np.asarray(vtop, dtype=np.int64))))
            merged = merged[np.argsort(-v[merged])]
            new_active = [int(x) for x in merged[:150]]
            if new_active == active:
                break
            active = new_active
        return a, False
    except Exception:
        return a, False


def lp_step_mincross(a, limit_s, gstate):
    try:
        a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
        n = int(a.size)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if n == 0 or s <= 0:
            return a, False
        start = time.monotonic()
        active = top_lags(fft_self_conv(a / s), 80, 0.0)
        prev_active = None
        for _ in range(6):
            if time.monotonic() - start > limit_s:
                break
            p = a / s
            cp = fft_self_conv(p)
            M = float(np.max(cp))
            if M <= 0.0:
                break
            remaining = max(5.0, limit_s - (time.monotonic() - start))
            solve_safe = min(35.0, remaining)
            z = solve_minmax_lp(p, active, 150, solve_safe)
            if z is None:
                break
            t = float(z[0])
            q = z[1:]
            qs = float(np.sum(q))
            if qs <= 1e-12:
                break
            qdir = (q / qs) * s
            best_a, best_sc, best_alpha = lp_line_search(a, qdir)
            if best_alpha > 0.0:
                update_global(gstate, best_a, "lp_mincross")
                return best_a, True
            v = fft_conv(p, q)
            v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
            vmax = float(np.max(v))
            tol = 1e-10 * max(1.0, M)
            if vmax <= t + tol:
                break
            viol = np.where(v > t + tol)[0]
            if viol.size == 0:
                break
            vtop = viol[np.argsort(-v[viol])][:150]
            merged = np.unique(np.concatenate((np.asarray(active, dtype=np.int64),
                                               np.asarray(vtop, dtype=np.int64))))
            merged = merged[np.argsort(-v[merged])]
            new_active = [int(x) for x in merged[:150]]
            if prev_active is not None and new_active == prev_active:
                break
            active = new_active
            prev_active = active
        return a, False
    except Exception:
        return a, False


def lp_step(a, limit_s, gstate):
    if not HAS_LP:
        return a, False
    if limit_s < 20.0:
        limit_s = 20.0
    first = 0.5 * limit_s
    if first < 10.0:
        first = max(10.0, 0.35 * limit_s)
    a2, imp2 = lp_step_maxsum(a, first, gstate)
    if imp2:
        return a2, True
    rest = max(10.0, limit_s - first)
    a3, imp3 = lp_step_mincross(a2 if imp2 else a, rest, gstate)
    if imp3:
        return a3, True
    return a, False


def smooth_step(a, state, gstate):
    a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    n = int(a.size)
    sc = fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        ba = gstate.get("best_a")
        if ba is not None and int(ba.size) == n:
            a = normalize_sum(ba, SUM_TARGET)
        else:
            a = np.full(n, SUM_TARGET / float(max(1, n)), dtype=np.float64)
        sc = fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return a, float("inf"), False, state
    update_global(gstate, a, "smooth")

    c = fft_self_conv(a)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return a, sc, True, state

    x = (c - M) / (M + 1e-15)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.minimum(x, 0.0)
    x_range = float(np.max(x) - np.min(x))
    tau = float(state.get("tau", 30.0))
    if x_range < 1e-8:
        w = np.full(c.size, 1.0 / float(c.size), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w = np.exp(tau * x)
        w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0.0)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if ws <= 1e-15:
            w = np.full(c.size, 1.0 / float(c.size), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w = w / ws

    g = compute_grad_smooth(w, a)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    ema = state.get("g_ema")
    if ema is None or int(np.asarray(ema).size) != n:
        state["g_ema"] = g.copy()
    else:
        state["g_ema"] = 0.65 * np.asarray(ema, dtype=np.float64) + 0.35 * g
    g = state["g_ema"]
    gmax = float(np.max(g))
    if gmax <= 1e-300:
        return a, sc, False, state
    gn = g / gmax
    gn = np.minimum(gn, 2.0)

    eta = float(state.get("eta", 0.08))
    floor = 1e-12 * SUM_TARGET / float(max(1, n))
    improved = False
    exp1 = np.exp(-eta * gn)
    cand1 = a * exp1
    cand1 = np.maximum(cand1, floor)
    cand1 = normalize_sum(cand1, SUM_TARGET)
    sc1 = fast_score(cand1)
    if np.isfinite(sc1) and sc1 < sc - 1e-11:
        a = cand1
        sc = sc1
        improved = True
        eta = float(min(max(eta * 1.08, 1e-4), 0.8))
        state["no_improve"] = 0
    else:
        if eta > 0.008:
            exp2 = np.exp(-0.5 * eta * gn)
            cand2 = a * exp2
            cand2 = np.maximum(cand2, floor)
            cand2 = normalize_sum(cand2, SUM_TARGET)
            sc2 = fast_score(cand2)
            if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < sc - 1e-11:
                a = cand2
                sc = sc2
                improved = True
                eta = float(max(0.55 * eta, 1e-4))
                state["no_improve"] = 0
            else:
                eta = float(max(0.7 * eta, 1e-4))
                state["no_improve"] = int(state.get("no_improve", 0)) + 1
        else:
            eta = float(max(0.8 * eta, 1e-5))
            state["no_improve"] = int(state.get("no_improve", 0)) + 1

    state["eta"] = eta
    if int(state.get("no_improve", 0)) > 25:
        state["tau"] = float(min(float(state.get("tau", 30.0)) * 1.15, 500.0))
    if int(state.get("no_improve", 0)) > 140:
        state["tau"] = max(15.0, float(state.get("tau", 30.0)) * 0.8)
    state["iter"] = int(state.get("iter", 0)) + 1
    update_global(gstate, a, "smooth")
    return a, sc, improved, state


def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    n = int(a.size)
    noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), n))
    cand = a * noise
    cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
    return normalize_sum(cand, SUM_TARGET)


def phase_search(a, limit_s, gstate):
    """
    Conservative iterative Fourier phase search: sample the current sequence,
    shave large autocorrelation peaks, update the spectrum phase, project back
    to nonnegative support.  Uses the sequence only when a line search improves
    the objective.
    """
    try:
        a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
        n = int(a.size)
        s = float(a.sum())
        deadline = time.monotonic() + float(limit_s)
        L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
        improved = False
        factors = (0.99, 0.98, 0.97, 0.96)

        for factor in factors:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            for _ in range(6):
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    break
                p = a / s
                pad = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
                pad[:n] = p
                P = np.fft.rfft(pad, L)
                c = np.fft.irfft(P * P, L)[:2 * n - 1]
                c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
                c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
                M = float(np.max(c))
                if M <= 0.0:
                    break
                cap = M * float(factor)
                cn = c.copy()
                mask = cn > cap
                excess = float(np.sum(cn[mask])) - cap * float(np.count_nonzero(mask))
                if excess <= 0.0:
                    break
                cn[mask] = cap
                head = cap - cn
                headsum = float(np.sum(head))
                if headsum > 1e-15:
                    cn += (excess / headsum) * head
                    cn = np.maximum(cn, 0.0)
                    snew = float(np.sum(cn))
                    if snew > 1e-15:
                        cn /= snew
                elif float(np.sum(cn)) > 1e-15:
                    cn /= float(np.sum(cn))
                else:
                    break
                C = np.fft.rfft(cn, L)
                mag = np.sqrt(np.abs(C))
                Ap = np.abs(P)
                phase = np.ones(P.shape, dtype=np.complex128)
                mp = Ap > 1e-300
                phase[mp] = P[mp] / Ap[mp]
                Pn = mag * phase
                qfull = np.fft.irfft(Pn, L)
                q = qfull[:n]
                q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
                q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
                qs = float(np.sum(q))
                if qs <= 1e-12:
                    break
                qdir = (q / qs) * s
                a0 = a
                sc0 = fast_score(a0)
                best_a = a0
                best_sc = sc0
                local_improved = False
                for alpha in (0.10, 0.30, 0.50, 0.80):
                    cand = (1.0 - alpha) * a0 + alpha * qdir
                    cand = normalize_sum(cand, SUM_TARGET)
                    sc = fast_score(cand)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-11:
                        best_a = cand
                        best_sc = sc
                        local_improved = True
                if local_improved:
                    a = best_a
                    improved = True
                    update_global(gstate, a, "phase")
                else:
                    break
        return a, improved
    except Exception:
        return a, False


def make_parametric(n):
    n = int(max(16, n))
    u = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    shapes = []
    shapes.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    shapes.append(1.0 + 0.4 * u)
    shapes.append(1.0 - 0.4 * u)
    shapes.append(np.sqrt(u + 1e-8) + np.sqrt(1.0 - u + 1e-8))
    shapes.append(np.exp(-8.0 * (u - 0.5) ** 2))
    shapes.append(0.5 + 0.5 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * u))
    shapes.append(0.5 + 0.5 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * u + 0.5))
    shapes.append(1.0 + 0.3 * np.sin(4.0 * np.pi * u))
    out = []
    for sh in shapes:
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(sh), sh, 0.0)
        arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
        out.append(normalize_sum(arr, SUM_TARGET))
    return out


def optimize_candidate(a, budget, gstate, seed):
    a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)
    n = int(a.size)
    state = {"eta": 0.08, "tau": 30.0, "no_improve": 0, "iter": 0, "g_ema": None}
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(budget)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    reserve = 25.0
    last_improve = time.monotonic()
    next_lp = time.monotonic() + 120.0
    next_phase = time.monotonic() + 540.0

    while time.monotonic() + reserve < deadline:
        now = time.monotonic()
        stagnant = now - last_improve

        ha = gstate.get("best_a")
        if ha is not None and int(ha.size) == n:
            ba = np.asarray(ha, dtype=np.float64)
            bsc = fast_score(ba)
            csc = fast_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(bsc) and (not np.isfinite(csc) or bsc < csc - 1e-7):
                a = normalize_sum(ba, SUM_TARGET)
                state = {"eta": 0.08, "tau": 30.0, "no_improve": 0, "iter": 0, "g_ema": None}
                last_improve = now

        if HAS_LP and now > next_lp and time.monotonic() + reserve + 45.0 < deadline:
            lp_budget = min(90.0, max(20.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - reserve - 10.0))
            a, imp = lp_step(a, lp_budget, gstate)
            next_lp = time.monotonic() + 240.0
            if imp:
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                state = {"eta": 0.08, "tau": 30.0, "no_improve": 0, "iter": 0, "g_ema": None}
        elif stagnant > 540.0 and now > next_phase and time.monotonic() + reserve + 25.0 < deadline:
            ph_budget = min(60.0, max(10.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - reserve - 10.0))
            a, imp = phase_search(a, ph_budget, gstate)
            next_phase = time.monotonic() + 800.0
            if imp:
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                state = {"eta": 0.08, "tau": 30.0, "no_improve": 0, "iter": 0, "g_ema": None}
            else:
                base = a
                ha2 = gstate.get("best_a")
                if ha2 is not None and int(ha2.size) == n:
                    base = normalize_sum(ha2, SUM_TARGET)
                sc_base = fast_score(base)
                best_cand = base
                best_cand_sc = sc_base
                for sig in (0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.60):
                    cand = random_perturb(base, rng, sig)
                    csc = fast_score(cand)
                    if np.isfinite(csc) and csc < best_cand_sc - 1e-7:
                        best_cand = cand
                        best_cand_sc = csc
                a = best_cand
                state = {"eta": 0.09, "tau": 25.0, "no_improve": 0, "iter": 0, "g_ema": None}
                update_global(gstate, a, "random")
        else:
            a, sc, imp, state = smooth_step(a, state, gstate)
            if imp:
                last_improve = time.monotonic()

    ha = gstate.get("best_a")
    if ha is not None and int(ha.size) == n:
        a = normalize_sum(ha, SUM_TARGET)
    else:
        a = normalize_sum(a, SUM_TARGET)

    state["eta"] = 0.004
    state["tau"] = max(15.0, min(180.0, float(state.get("tau", 30.0)) * 0.55))
    state["no_improve"] = 0

    while time.monotonic() + 2.0 < deadline:
        a, sc, imp, state = smooth_step(a, state, gstate)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            break
    return a


def prepare_final(a):
    a = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        a = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        a = np.full(a.size, SUM_TARGET / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a * (SUM_TARGET / s)
    mx = float(np.max(a))
    if mx > 1000.0:
        a = a * (1000.0 / mx)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        a = np.full(a.size, SUM_TARGET / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a


def propose_candidate(seed=42, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for a low-score non-negative sequence and return it as a Python list.
    """
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    search_deadline = start_time + 2350.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    gstate = {
        "best_score": float("inf"),
        "best_a": None,
        "best_tag": "init",
        "pool": [],
        "exact_score": float("inf"),
        "exact_a": None,
        "exact_tag": ""
    }

    parent = get_parent()
    base = normalize_sum(parent, SUM_TARGET)
    if int(base.size) > MAX_LEN:
        base = interpolate_mass(base, MAX_LEN)

    candidates = []
    candidates.append((base, "parent"))

    n0 = int(base.size)
    for lp in (2048, 4096, MAX_LEN):
        if lp <= MAX_LEN and lp != n0:
            candidates.append((interpolate_mass(base, lp), "interp_%d" % lp))

    param_len = MAX_LEN
    for pa in make_parametric(param_len):
        candidates.append((pa, "param"))

    scored = []
    for a, tag in candidates:
        sc = fast_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((float(sc), a, tag))

    if not scored:
        a = normalize_sum(np.ones(1024), SUM_TARGET)
        return [float(x) for x in prepare_final(a)]

    scores = np.empty(len(scored), dtype=np.float64)
    for i, item in enumerate(scored):
        scores[i] = item[0]
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    scored = [scored[i] for i in order]

    for sc, a, tag in scored[:5]:
        update_global(gstate, a, tag)

    targets = [scored[0]]
    for i in range(1, len(scored)):
        sc, a, tag = scored[i]
        if a.size != scored[0][1].size or sc < scored[0][0] + 0.02:
            targets.append(scored[i])
            break

    # Exact-validate a few initial candidates before expensive search.
    init_exact_list = []
    for item in targets:
        init_exact_list.append(item)
        if len(init_exact_list) >= 3:
            break
    if len(scored) > 1 and len(init_exact_list) < 4:
        init_exact_list.append(scored[1])
    seen_init = set()
    for sc, a, tag in init_exact_list:
        aa = prepare_final(a)
        key = (int(aa.size), tuple(np.round(aa[:min(8, aa.size)], 3).tolist()))
        if key in seen_init:
            continue
        seen_init.add(key)
        ev = exact_score_array(aa)
        if np.isfinite(ev) and ev < float(gstate["exact_score"]):
            gstate["exact_score"] = float(ev)
            gstate["exact_a"] = aa.copy()
            gstate["exact_tag"] = tag

    a0, tag0 = targets[0][1], targets[0][2]
    remaining = max(0.0, search_deadline - time.monotonic())
    b1 = b2 = 0.0
    if len(targets) > 1:
        s0 = targets[0][0]
        s1 = targets[1][0]
        if s1 < s0 + 0.005:
            b1 = 0.45 * remaining
            b2 = 0.45 * remaining
        elif s1 < s0 + 0.030:
            b1 = 0.60 * remaining
            b2 = 0.35 * remaining
        else:
            b1 = 0.80 * remaining
            b2 = 0.15 * remaining
    else:
        b1 = remaining

    opt1 = optimize_candidate(a0.copy(), b1, gstate, int(seed))
    opt2 = None
    if len(targets) > 1 and b2 > 20.0 and time.monotonic() < search_deadline:
        opt2 = optimize_candidate(targets[1][1].copy(), b2, gstate, int(seed) + 917)

    # Final exact validation of the best candidates.
    final_check = []
    if gstate.get("best_a") is not None:
        final_check.append(gstate["best_a"])
    if gstate.get("exact_a") is not None:
        final_check.append(gstate["exact_a"])
    for item in gstate.get("pool", []):
        final_check.append(item[1])
    if opt2 is not None:
        final_check.append(opt2)

    seen = set()
    for aa in final_check:
        if aa is None:
            continue
        if time.monotonic() > start_time + 2380.0:
            break
        arr = prepare_final(aa)
        key = (int(arr.size), tuple(np.round(arr[:min(10, arr.size)], 3).tolist()))
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        ev = exact_score_array(arr)
        if np.isfinite(ev) and ev < float(gstate["exact_score"]):
            gstate["exact_score"] = float(ev)
            gstate["exact_a"] = arr.copy()

    final_a = gstate.get("exact_a")
    if final_a is None:
        final_a = gstate.get("best_a")
    if final_a is None:
        final_a = base
    final_a = prepare_final(final_a)

    final_ev = exact_score_array(final_a)
    if np.isfinite(final_ev) and final_ev < float(gstate["exact_score"]):
        gstate["exact_score"] = float(final_ev)
        gstate["exact_a"] = final_a.copy()

    print("final n=%d exact_score=%.10f" % (int(final_a.size),
                                              gstate["exact_score"] if np.isfinite(gstate["exact_score"]) else float("inf")))
    return [float(x) for x in final_a]
```
